## Northern Mariana Islands § 6930.pdf

*Citation: Commonwealth Code, Title 1, Division 6 — Elections, § 6930.pdf. Last changed: 2026-08-06. Source: https://cnmilaw.org/pdf/cmc_section/T1/6930.pdf*

TITLE 1: GOVERNMENT  
DIVISION 6: ELECTIONS 
 
§ 6930.  Functions and Duties.  
The Mayor -elect shall be staffed by s uch temporary employees as are 
appointed by the Mayor-elect. The office shall work together with the incumbent 
Mayor or designee and the current officials to provide for the orderly transfer of 
power from one Mayor to the next. The incumbent Mayor or desig nee and 
current officials of the Office of the Mayor shall cooperate with the office of the 
Mayor-elect to the maximum extent practical. 
Source: PL 19-11 § 3(101) (Oct. 15, 2015), modified. 
Commission Comment:  The Commission numbered the sections in this 
chapter pursuant to 1 CMC § 3806(a). 
PL 19 -11 (Oct.  15, 2015) included, in addition to severability and savings 
clauses, the following Short Title and Findings and Purpose sections: 
 
Section 1. Short Title.  This chapter may be cited as the “Mayoral 
Transition Act of 2015.” 
Section 2. Findings and Purpose.  The Commonwealth Legislature 
finds that there are currently no provisions or procedures for a successful 
transition for a Mayor -elect into office. Due to the v oluminous data 
acquired throughout the mayoral term of office, it is imperative that the 
incumbent Mayor work collaboratively with the Mayor -Elect in order to 
efficiently continue providing public service to the community. 
The purpose of this Act is to pro vide a healthy closure with the 
incumbent Mayor, provide a sufficient timeframe to review and plan 
closed and ongoing projects, and to review the inventory of fixed assets 
and ca pital goods beginning two weeks after certification from the 
Commonwealth Elec tion Commission, and to assure a successful 
transition in mayoral leadership. In addition, this Act imposes a penalty 
for a violation of a certain provision of the Act.

## Northern Mariana Islands § 6931.pdf

*Citation: Commonwealth Code, Title 1, Division 6 — Elections, § 6931.pdf. Last changed: 2026-08-06. Source: https://cnmilaw.org/pdf/cmc_section/T1/6931.pdf*

TITLE 1: GOVERNMENT  
DIVISION 6: ELECTIONS 
 
§ 6931.  Access to Information.  
To further cooperation, the head of each department, office, gaming 
commission excluding the Commonwealth Lottery Commission, instrumentality, 
or other entity of the Office of the Mayor shall upon request: 
(a) Make available all files and records, except personal working files, any 
confidential files of pers ons appointed by the Mayor, and any files, such as tax 
records, to which access is prohibited by law; 
(b) Extract information from such files and records in response to reasonably 
specific requests; 
(c) Make available sufficient staff to carry out these duties within a reasonable 
time period to serve as liaison to the office of the Mayor -elect; provided, that the 
Mayor-elect or a designee may request that specific individuals be assigned for 
this purpose, but that the final decision regarding the assignment  shall rest with 
the head of the entity; and 
(d) Provide an inventory of all fixed assets and capital goods, including but not 
limited to office and field equipment, office furnishings and supplies, etc. The 
inventory shall provide specific description of the item, quantity, tag number, 
location, and item value. 
Source: PL 19-11 § 3(102) (Oct. 15, 2015). 
Commission Comment: The Commission changed a colon to a period in 
the section title, pursuant to 1 CMC § 3806(g).

## Northern Mariana Islands § 6932.pdf

*Citation: Commonwealth Code, Title 1, Division 6 — Elections, § 6932.pdf. Last changed: 2026-08-06. Source: https://cnmilaw.org/pdf/cmc_section/T1/6932.pdf*

TITLE 1: GOVERNMENT  
DIVISION 6: ELECTIONS 
 
§ 6932.  Employees.  
The Mayor -elect may  engage the services of private firms, experts, 
consultants, and other professionals and may employ such staff as necessary to 
carry out the purposes of this chapter. No person employed under this section 
shall become a member of the civil service by virtue of such employment. 
 
Source: PL 19-11 § 3(103) (Oct. 15, 2015).

## Northern Mariana Islands § 6933.pdf

*Citation: Commonwealth Code, Title 1, Division 6 — Elections, § 6933.pdf. Last changed: 2026-08-06. Source: https://cnmilaw.org/pdf/cmc_section/T1/6933.pdf*

TITLE 1: GOVERNMENT  
DIVISION 6: ELECTIONS 
 
§ 6933.  Committee on Transition.  
(a) For the purpose of carrying out the duties of the Mayor -elect, the Mayor -
elect may establish a committee on transition, the member of which shall be 
appointed by and serve at the pleasure of the Mayor-elect; 
(b) Any branch, office, agency, board, commission, instrumentality, or other 
entity of the Commonwealth government which employs any person appointed to 
the committee may assign, as available without substantial interference with the 
routine administration of government, the person on reimbursable or non -
reimbursable detail to the committee for such time as the Mayor -elect may 
request. While so detailed, the employee shall be responsible only to the Ma yor-
elect for the performance of his or her duties; provided, that any employee so 
detailed shall continue to receive the compensation provided pursuant to law for 
regular employment, and shall retain the rights and privileges of his or her 
employment without interruption. Such persons shall not receive any additional 
compensation by virtue of their service on the committee; 
(c) Any person appointed to the committee who is not subject to detail 
pursuant to subsection (b) of this section may be compensated p ursuant to short-
term employment contracts for service actually rendered at an appropriate rate to 
be determined by the Mayor -elect pursuant to budgetary appropriation. No such 
person shall become a member of the civil service by virtue of this appointment; 
(d) The committee may be assisted by persons of its own choosing who shall 
not be compensated for their services, but who may be reimbursed for reasonable 
and necessary expenses incident to their assistance; 
(e) The Mayor may make clerical and administra tive staff available to the 
Mayor-elect on the same basis as provided in subsection (b) of this section. 
Source: PL 19-11 § 3(104) (Oct. 15, 2015). 
 
Commission Comment: The Commission changed a colon to a period in         
the section title, pursuant to 1 CMC § 3806(g).

## Northern Mariana Islands § 6934.pdf

*Citation: Commonwealth Code, Title 1, Division 6 — Elections, § 6934.pdf. Last changed: 2026-08-06. Source: https://cnmilaw.org/pdf/cmc_section/T1/6934.pdf*

TITLE 1: GOVERNMENT  
DIVISION 6: ELECTIONS 
 
§ 6934.  Facilities.  
The incumbent Mayor or designee shall provide, upon request, to the Mayor -
elect for use in connection with the preparation for the assumption of official 
duties as Mayor, necessary services and facilities, including suitable off ice space 
appropriately equipped with furniture, furnishings, and office supplies as 
determined by the incumbent Mayor or designee after consultation with the 
Mayor-elect, within the government complex. If the office space provided is not 
adequate, the May or-elect may execute a short -term lease agreement for office 
space. 
 
Source: PL 19-11 § 3(105) (Oct. 15, 2015).

## Northern Mariana Islands § 6935.pdf

*Citation: Commonwealth Code, Title 1, Division 6 — Elections, § 6935.pdf. Last changed: 2026-08-06. Source: https://cnmilaw.org/pdf/cmc_section/T1/6935.pdf*

TITLE 1: GOVERNMENT  
DIVISION 6: ELECTIONS 
 
§ 6935.  Inauguration Ceremonies.  
The Mayor -elect shall provide for the inauguration of the new Mayor in 
appropriate ceremonies. 
Source: PL 19-11 § 3(106) (Oct. 15, 2015).

## Northern Mariana Islands § 6936.pdf

*Citation: Commonwealth Code, Title 1, Division 6 — Elections, § 6936.pdf. Last changed: 2026-08-06. Source: https://cnmilaw.org/pdf/cmc_section/T1/6936.pdf*

TITLE 1: GOVERNMENT  
DIVISION 6: ELECTIONS 
 
§ 6936.  Termination of Activities.  
The Mayor -elect shall cease operations upon the inauguration of the new 
Mayor; provided, that the staff shall prepare and submit a financial summary and 
activities report to the Mayor and the legislature 20 days after the inauguration. 
Source: PL 19-11 § 3(107) (Oct. 15, 2015). 
The Mayor -elect shall provide for the inauguration of the new Mayor in 
appropriate ceremonies. 
Source: PL 19-11 § 3(106) (Oct. 15, 2015).

## Northern Mariana Islands § 6937.pdf

*Citation: Commonwealth Code, Title 1, Division 6 — Elections, § 6937.pdf. Last changed: 2026-08-06. Source: https://cnmilaw.org/pdf/cmc_section/T1/6937.pdf*

TITLE 1: GOVERNMENT  
DIVISION 6: ELECTIONS 
 
§ 6937.  Appropriations and Reprogramming.  
The Mayor shall include in his/her budget submission to the legislature, for 
each fiscal year in which his regular t erm of office will expire, an identified 
appropriation for use under this chapter shall remain available for any mayoral 
transition during the fiscal year in which the transition occurs. The Secretary of 
Finance is authorized to expend all funds necessary to carry out the purposes of 
this chapter. In the event the legislature fails to appropriate the necessary funds to 
carry out the purposes of this chapter, the Mayor shall reprogram resources 
appropriated to the respective senatorial departments so that th e purposes of this 
chapter may be achieved. 
 
Source: PL 19-11 § 3(108) (Oct. 15, 2015).

## Northern Mariana Islands § 6938.pdf

*Citation: Commonwealth Code, Title 1, Division 6 — Elections, § 6938.pdf. Last changed: 2026-08-06. Source: https://cnmilaw.org/pdf/cmc_section/T1/6938.pdf*

TITLE 1: GOVERNMENT  
DIVISION 6: ELECTIONS 
 
§ 6938.  Fixed Assets and Capital Goods of the Office of the Mayor.  
All fixed assets and capital goods situated in Office of the Mayor, listed in the 
inventory as provided under 1 CMC § 6931(d), shall not be transferred out of the 
office. The fixed assets and capital goods shall remain under the custody and 
possession of the Office of the Mayor to be used by the Mayor-elect. 
Source: PL 19-11 § 3(109) (Oct. 15, 2015), modified. 
Commission Comment: The Commission changed “section 102(d)” to “1 
CMC § 6931(d)” pursuant to 1 CMC § 3806(c).

## Northern Mariana Islands § 6939.pdf

*Citation: Commonwealth Code, Title 1, Division 6 — Elections, § 6939.pdf. Last changed: 2026-08-06. Source: https://cnmilaw.org/pdf/cmc_section/T1/6939.pdf*

TITLE 1: GOVERNMENT  
DIVISION 6: ELECTIONS 
 
§ 6939.  Penalty.  
(a) Except as provided in subsection (b) of this section, any person found by a 
court to be guilty of knowingly violatin g any of the provisions of this chapter or 
furnishing false, misleading or incomplete information to the Mayor -elect or his 
office with the intent to mislead, upon conviction thereof shall be punished by 
imprisonment of not more than ninety days or a fine of not more than $5,000 for 
each offense, or both; 
(b) The incumbent Mayor or designee shall be personally liable for violating 1 
CMC § 6938 and the penalty shall be in a sum equal to the value to replace the 
property for the items that are not accounted for as listed in the inventory of fixed 
assets and capital goods as provided under 1 CMC § 6931(d). The civil penalty 
shall be assessed by a judge of the Commonwealth Superior Court and an action 
to enforce this penalty may be brought by the new Mayor. 
Source: PL 19-11 § 3(110) (Oct. 15, 2015), modified. 
Commission Comment:  The Commission changed “section 109” and 
“section 102(d)” in subsection (b) to “1 CMC § 6938” and “1 CMC § 6931(d)” 
respectively, pursuant to 1 CMC § 3806(c). The Commission changed a colon 
to a period in the section title, pursuant to 1 CMC § 3806(g).

Northern Mariana Islands 
(Commonwealth) Local Laws: First 
Senatorial District (Rota) 
Commission Comment : Senatorial 
district delega tions of the Commonwealth 
Legislature enact local laws pursuant to the 
Local Law Act of 1983 (1 CMC §§ 1401 et 
seq.), which permi ts legislation pertaining 
exclusively to individual senatorial districts, 
of which there are three: the First Senatorial 
District (Rota), the S econd Senatorial 
District (Tini an and Aguiguan), and the 
Third Senatorial District (Saipan and 
Northern Islands). 
Initially, local laws were designated by 
order of enactment regardless of the 
senatorial district to which they applied. 
(For example, Local Law 6-1, applying only 
to Saipan and the Northern Islands, was 
followed by Local Law 6 -2, applying only 
to Rota, fo llowed by Local Law 6 -3, 
applying only to Tinian and Aguiguan.) 
Beginning with the Eighth Commonwealth 
Legislature in 1992, local laws were 
expressly designated by the senatorial 
district to which they applied and numbered 
sequentially for each district, regardless of 
order of enactment among all local laws. 
Uncodified temporary laws are not 
included in this compilation.
